People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I know it sounds like people don’t believe me, but I’m sure they’re watching when I come and go, and those “notices” on the doors… I think some of them are notes about me going to the office.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble taking care of your daily activities, like cleaning or paying bills, since you started feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. People there are watching when I leave and come back, and I’m pretty sure they’re passing notes about me to the office. They keep saying it’s just notices on the doors, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really. I hear noises in the building sometimes, but I can’t say if other people hear them or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to organize your thoughts or express them clearly to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I’ll try to explain something and it comes out all mixed up. Like I start talking about the rent, then I’m on about the marina, then the choir robes, and the wires in the walls… and people look at me like I left half of it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that people in your apartment are watching you and passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I know it sounds like people don’t believe me, but I’m sure they’re watching when I come and go, and those “notices” on the doors… I think some of them are notes about me going to the office.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble taking care of your daily activities, like cleaning or paying bills, since you started feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. I’ve let bills sit too long, and I’ve missed appointments. I just don’t keep the days straight like I should. I stopped going to church things and helping out too… it got harder to deal with people and know what’s just normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty completing tasks due to being easily distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’ll sit down with bills or those appointment cards, and I keep reading the same line over and over. Then I still can’t figure out what I’m supposed to do first, so it just… piles up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting in ways that are unusual for you, like doing things that don't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah. At the senior-center workshop, I was supposed to be fixing a shelf, but I just kept lining up the screws by size and tapping on the table. When the lady told me to sit down, I just stood there holding the screwdriver… like I couldn’t move on to the next thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself engaging in activities without a clear purpose or goal?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. Like at that workshop, I was supposed to fix a shelf, but I just ended up lining the screws up and tapping on the table. Then when she told me to sit down, I just kind of stood there holding the screwdriver… couldn’t get myself to move on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble staying on topic when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I’ll try to explain something and I start with, like, the rent, then I’m talking about the marina, then choir robes or wires in the walls… and people look at me like I left parts out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're living in a dream or that things around you aren't real?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. Sometimes things feel off, but I wouldn’t say like a dream. I’m not sure what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you've lost touch with reality?\n\nPatient: I mean… people tell me I’m reading it wrong, but I don’t think so. The people in my building, they watch when I come and go, and they put notes about me to the office. Everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, but… I know what it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that are fragmented or hard to put into words?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it happens. I’ll be trying to explain one thing, like the rent, and then it jumps to the marina or the choir robes, or the wires in the walls. People act like I skipped parts, but it makes sense to me at the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're not in control of your own actions or thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like somebody’s moving me around or putting thoughts in my head. My mind gets busy, but it still feels like mine, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble putting your thoughts together in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I try to explain something, like the rent, and then I’m talking about the marina, or the choir robes, or the wires in the walls. People look at me like I left out half the story.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're in a different world or reality than everyone else?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels that way, yeah. Like at my apartment… people keep saying it’s just notices on the doors, but I know they’re watching when I come and go, and slipping notes about me to the office. I don’t know… it just feels like I’m the only one seeing what’s really going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're completely detached from the world around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… sometimes I feel kind of distant, like everything’s going on without me, but I don’t know if I’d call it completely detached.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that don't make sense even to yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I’ll be trying to think it through, like the rent, and then my mind goes to the marina, or the choir robes, or the wires in the walls. Even I don’t always know how I got there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to stick to one topic when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’ll start trying to explain one thing, like the rent, and then I’m talking about the marina or the choir robes, or the wires in the walls. People look at me like I left parts out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices that other people cannot hear lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really sure. Sometimes I hear things, but I can’t tell if it’s just noise or my own thoughts. I don’t want to say it wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a logical and coherent way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I’ll start trying to explain something, like the rent, and then I’m off talking about the marina, or the choir robes, or the wires in the walls. People look at me like I skipped a bunch of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
